<meta>This page continues the astrological and mythological explanations of the planet Jupiter's influence on human love and friendship. The previous page discussed Jupiter's interaction with Saturn and Capricorn (the Satyr) and gendered influences (Callisto and Ganymede).</meta>

**Jupiter** is friendly in himself, and inclines those under his influence to the friendship of love; and, although his love is virtuous <note>original: "honesto" — in Renaissance philosophy, this refers to noble, virtuous, or honorable love as opposed to purely carnal love.</note>, nonetheless those who have his influence in their nativity <note>A birth chart or the specific alignment of planets at the moment of birth.</note> (who are called "sons of **Jupiter**" by the poets), through the interaction with each of the other planets, he makes them lovers of virtuous things, mixed with those qualities belonging to the nature of whichever planet they have in company. Hence he sometimes [gives] a love that is clean, pure, clear, manifest, and sweet, according to his own Jovial nature. And in this manner, they feign <note>original: "fingano" — meaning the poets create allegorical fictions to explain natural or divine truths.</note> that he loved **Leda**, and that he joined with her in the form of a **Swan**, because the swan is white, clean, clear, and of sweet song; for which reason **Leda** took him, and then found herself taken by him, and gave birth by him to **Castor** and **Pollux** in one birth, who were called sons of **Jupiter** because they were excellent in virtue. And so too was **Helen**, for her clear beauty like that of a swan; and her two brothers were converted by **Jupiter** into the sign of **Gemini** <note>The Twins.</note>, as it is the house of **Mercury**, which signifies sweet eloquence through the sweet song of the swan, denoting that purity of the soul, with sweetness of speech, is a great cause of love and friendship.

At other times, **Jupiter** gives his virtuous love in a way that is not so apparent and manifest, but rather cloudy, internal, and covered; and therefore they say that he loved the daughter of **Inachus** <note>Io.</note>, whom he possessed in the form of a cloud. And if **Jupiter** has interaction with **Venus**, he makes love tend toward the pleasurable. Whence they posit that he loved and obtained **Europa** in the form of a beautiful **Bull**, because the sign of **Taurus** <note>The Bull.</note> is the domicile of **Venus**. 

And if he has interaction with **Mercury**, he gives a love tending toward the useful, because **Mercury** is the provider <note>original: "procuratore" — an agent, steward, or manager.</note> of substances. For this reason, they say that he loved and enjoyed **Danaë** in the form of a shower of **Gold**, because the liberal distribution of riches causes a man to be loved by those in need who receive them, like a rain of gold. 

And having a commission with the **Sun**, he gives love of state, of dominion, and of great heights. This they signify by positing that he loved and joined with **Asteria** in the form of an **Eagle**. 

And mixing with the **Moon**, he makes a love that is tender and pious, like that of a mother or a nurse for a small child. Whence they say that he loved and obtained **Semele**, daughter of **Cadmus**, in the form of **Beroe**, her nurse. 

And being tempered with **Mars**, he makes a love that is hot, fiery, and burning. And in such a manner, they say that he loved and obtained **Aegina** in the form of **Lightning**. 

And having company with **Saturn**, he makes a...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Jupiter’s various mythological transformations as allegories for his astrological influence when combined with other planets or signs. It transitions from the loves of Jupiter to the nature of Venus and her relationship with Mars.</meta>

love mixed with the honorable and the base, partly human and intellectual, and partly crude and unclean. Hence they feign that he loved and possessed **Antiope** in the form of a **Satyr**, which has the upper part of a man and the lower part of a goat, because the sign of **Capricorn** is the house of **Saturn** <note>In traditional astrology, Saturn rules Capricorn; the goat-like nature of the Satyr represents the "cruder" influence of Saturn on Jupiter's love.</note>. Furthermore, if **Jupiter** is found in a feminine sign, he gives feminine love, and for this reason they say he loved and possessed **Callisto** in the form of a woman. And if he is found in a masculine sign, especially in the house of **Saturn**, that is **Aquarius**, he gives masculine love. Wherefore they feign that he loved the boy **Ganymede**, and that he converted him into **Aquarius**, the sign of **Saturn**. In all these loves, and others of **Jupiter**, I could tell you even fuller allegories, but I leave them (not being too important) to avoid length; it is enough for you to know that all his loves denote types of love and friendships that depend on the influence of **Jupiter** in those who are dominated by him in their nativities <note>A "nativity" is a person's birth chart or the alignment of the stars at their birth.</note>, which influence he gives sometimes alone, and sometimes accompanied in different signs of the heaven. This denotes the number of his children, and the stories of those who participated differently in the virtues of **Jupiter**, and the manners of such participations.

**S O P H I A .** We have spoken enough of the loves of **Jupiter**; tell me of that famous love of his son **Mars** with **Venus**.

**P H I L O N E .** You have already learned above of the birth of **Mars**, from the striking of the womb of **Juno**. Because the planet **Venus**, according to the ancients, follows in the middle after **Mars**, then follows **Mercury**, then the **Sun**, and then the **Moon**; but more modern astrologers place the **Sun** between **Mars** and **Venus**. Regarding this **Venus**, the poets feign various things. Sometimes they call her **Great Venus** <note>original: "Venere magna"</note>, and they apply to her all the most excellent and great things of nature, and they say she is the daughter of **Father Heaven** and **Mother Day** <note>original: "Die madre" — from the Latin 'Dies' (Day).</note>. They give her the **Heaven** for a father because **Venus** is one of the seven celestial planets, and they give her **Day** for a mother because **Venus** is very bright, and when she is the evening star, she prolongs the day. And they say she gave birth to the twin love of **Jupiter**, and likewise the three sisters called the **Graces**. This means that love in the lower beings proceeds from these two benign planets, called the **Fortunes** <note>In astrology, Jupiter and Venus are known as the "Benefics" or "Fortunes" because of their positive influence.</note>, from **Jupiter** who is the **Greater Fortune**, and from **Venus** who is the **Lesser Fortune**. And thus **Jupiter** takes the place of a father...

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Sophia and Philo, moving from the multifaceted loves of Jupiter to the specific nature of Venus and the type of love she represents. ...for masculine superiority and excellence, and Venus in the place of a mother, for being lesser, lower, and feminine. Furthermore, the love of **Jupiter** is honorable, perfect, and masculine, and that of **Venus** is pleasurable, carnal, imperfect, and feminine. Whence they imagine this **Love** <note>The speaker refers to Cupid/Eros, the personification of Love, born from these two deities.</note> born of both to be twin-like, composed of the honorable and the pleasurable, and also because true love must be twofold and reciprocal between two lovers. Therefore, they generate the **Graces** together, because love is never without grace on both sides. They say that this **Venus**, coming into the house of **Mars**, caused Furies <note>In this context, "Furies" refers to intense, uncontrolled passions or "madness" in love.</note> within it, signifying that when, in someone’s nativity <note>A person's birth chart in astrology.</note>, **Venus** is found in one of the two signs that are the houses of **Mars** in the heavens—that is, in **Aries** or in **Scorpio**—she generates furious lovers of burning love, due to the heat of **Mars**. It is the same when **Venus** is in "aspect" with **Mars**. They depict her girded with a belt when she enters into unions and weddings, to signify the great bond and inseparable link that **Venus** places between those joined in love. Among the animals, they associate the **Doves** with her, for being very dedicated to amorous marriage; and of the herbs, the **Myrtle**, both for its sweet scent and because it is always green like love; also because the leaves of the **Myrtle** grow in pairs, one after the other, because love is always twin and reciprocal. Furthermore, the fruit of the **Myrtle** is black, to denote that love gives melancholy and distressing fruit. Of the flowers, they give her the **Rose** for its beauty and scent, and also for being surrounded by sharp thorns, because love is surrounded by passion, pain, and stinging torments.

**S O P H I A .** Is that **Venus** who is depicted naked in the sea, swimming inside a conch shell, this same one?

**P H I L O .** In effect, the human **Venus** was only the daughter of **Jupiter** and **Dione**; and they imagine her to have been married to **Vulcan**, but in truth she was married to **Adonis**. Others believe that she was first effectively married to **Vulcan** and afterward to **Adonis**. She was Queen in **Cyprus** and so dedicated to concupiscible love <term>concupiscible: relating to strong sexual desire or carnal appetite</term> that she demonstrated and made it lawful for women to be public [prostitutes]. Because of her great beauty and shining appearance, she was called **Venus**, in the likeness of the brightness of that planet, it being esteemed that the Celestial **Venus** [the planet] influences this one [the woman] with not only great beauty, but also burning lewdness, according to her nature of causing pleasurable life and concupiscible generation in the lower world. Whence **Venus** in **Cyprus** was first adored as a Goddess and granted temples, but the poets, under the veil of this, have said many fictional things, which are images...

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Sophia and Philo, moving from the multifaceted nature of Venus to the specific allegorical interpretations of her birth and her relationship with the forces of time and decay.</meta>

...of the natural complexion and effects of celestial **Venus**, and her excellent virtues, are signified under the name of **Venus Magna** <note>Latin: "Great Venus," often representing the higher, intellectual form of love.</note>, daughter of **Heaven** and of **God**, as I have already told you. But as for her incitement to carnal lust, the poets demonstrate this by narrating another manner of her birth. They say that **Saturn** cut off the testicles of his father **Caelus** <note>The Italian text uses "Celio," referring to Caelus or Uranus, the personification of the sky.</note> with a sickle. Others say that it was **Jupiter** who cut them from his father **Saturn** with his own sickle and threw them into the sea; from the blood of these, together with the foam of the sea, **Venus** was born. For this reason, they depict her naked, within a seashell, in the sea.

SO. What is the allegory of this strange origin? 

PH. The testicles of **Caelus** are the generative power that descends from heaven into the lower world <note>The "lower world" refers to the sublunary sphere—our physical Earth—where things are born and die.</note>, of which **Venus** is the proper instrument, being the one who specifically grants appetite and generative power to animals. They say that **Saturn** cut them with a sickle because **Saturn**, in Greek, is called **Cronos** <note>original: "Cronos"</note>, which means **Time**. Time is the cause of generation in this lower world because the temporal things within it, not being eternal, must have a beginning and must be generated. This is because time corrupts the things that are subject to it, and everything that can be corrupted must first be generated. So time, signified by **Saturn**, brought generation from Heaven into the lower world—which is called the **Sea** because of its continuous mutation from one form into another—through the continuous cycle of generation and corruption. 

And this was done by "cutting the testicles with the sickle," because it is through corruption <note>In Renaissance philosophy, "corruption" isn't necessarily a bad thing; it refers to the breakdown of one form of matter so that a new life or form can be "generated."</note> that generation occurs in this lower world. Furthermore, the proper nature of **Saturn** is to corrupt, just as that of **Venus** is to generate; the latter is the cause of birth, and the former the cause of death. If things did not decay, new things could not be generated. Therefore, they say that **Saturn** with his sickle (with which he destroys and corrupts everything) cut the virile parts of **Caelus**, his father, and threw them into this mundane sea, from which **Venus** was generated. She represents the generative power of the lower realms, mixed with the power of corruption due to the cutting of **Caelus's** testicles. 

Those who say that the testicles which were cut were those of **Saturn**, from which **Venus** was born, signify that **Saturn** prohibits generation; for **Jupiter** cut off his testicles, whereby he remained incapable of generating. But the generative instruments that **Saturn** lost then formed **Venus**, who is the entire cause of generation. They also signify that **Saturn** is the planet that first (after intercourse) causes the

*generation, because it* <note>Referring to Saturn, mentioned on the previous page.</note> *causes the coagulation of the sperm, and for this reason, it dominates in the first month of pregnancy; but **Jupiter** immediately takes it away and assumes dominion over the conception, forming the creature in the second month, in which **Jupiter** dominates, and this is meant to signify the cutting of the testicles of the father **Saturn**. This means that the animal is generated from the sperm of the male—which is the blood of the testicles—and from the thin sperm of the woman, which is like foam* <note>This reflects the Galenic medical theory of the time, which held that both men and women produced "seed" or sperm necessary for conception.</note>*; or, by the foam, one understands the sperm of the man, which is so white, and by the blood, that of the woman, from which the creature is nourished. They paint her naked because love cannot be hidden, and also because she is carnal, and because lovers must find themselves naked. She swims in the sea because generative love extends throughout this entire world, which is continually changeable like the sea; also because love makes lovers restless, doubtful, vigilant, and stormy, like the sea.* **SO.** *I have understood enough of the origin and birth of **Venus**; it is now time that I know of her enamoring with **Mars**.***PH.** *They say that **Venus** was married to **Vulcan**, and because he was lame, she fell in love with **Mars**, bold and strenuous in arms, with whom she secretly consorted. She was seen by the **Sun** and accused to **Vulcan**, who placed invisible nets of iron around the bed where they both lay, and there they were found caught naked. Whereupon **Vulcan**, having called the gods—principally **Neptune**, **Mercury**, and **Apollo**—showed them **Mars** and **Venus** naked and caught in the iron nets. At this sight, the gods covered their faces for shame, but **Neptune** alone pleaded so much with **Vulcan** that, through his prayers, **Mars** and **Venus** were liberated. For this reason, **Venus** ever after hated the **Sun** and all his progeny, and for this, she caused all his daughters to commit adultery* <note>A reference to the mythological "Curse of Venus" on the daughters of Helios (the Sun), such as Pasiphae and Phaedra, as revenge for his whistleblowing.</note>. **SO.** *What do you say then, O **Philo**, of such lasciviousness and adultery among the celestial gods?***PH.** *Not only is the allegory of this fable scientific, but also useful, because it demonstrates that the excess of carnal lasciviousness not only condemns all the powers and virtues of the human body, but also causes a defect in the act itself, with a decrease from the ordinary.* **SO.** *Explain it to me at length.***PH.** ***Venus** is the concupiscible appetite...*

<meta>This page continues Philo’s explanation to Sophia regarding the allegorical and astrological nature of the gods. Having discussed the birth of Venus from the foam of the sea, Philo now moves to her relationship with Vulcan and her illicit affair with Mars, interpreting these myths as metaphors for human physiology and psychology.</meta>

...human appetite, which comes from **Venus**, who (according to the efficacy of her influence in births) is great and intense. This **Venus** is married to **Vulcan**, who is the God of the inferior fire; in man, this is his natural heat, which imitates and activates concupiscence <note>Concupiscence: strong sexual desire or lust.</note>, and as her husband, he is always actively joined to her. They say this **Vulcan** is the son of **Jupiter** and **Juno**, and because he was lame, they threw him from heaven, and he was nourished by **Thetis**, and he is the blacksmith of **Jupiter**, performing his craftsmanship. 

They mean to say that the natural heat of man, as well as that of animals, is the child of **Jupiter** and **Juno** because it has something of the celestial mixed with materiality. Through its participation in **Jupiter** and heaven, it is the subject of the natural, animal, and vital virtues; yet, by reason of the mixture it has with matter, it is not eternal like the actual heat of the **Sun** and the other celestial bodies. Nor is it always equally powerful, nor is it found in a constant state within the human body; rather, like a lame man, it grows and then decreases, rises and then falls, according to the diversity of age and the dispositions of man. And this is why it is said that, being lame, he was cast out of heaven: because heat and other celestial things do not "limp" <note>To "limp" or "stumble" here implies the irregularity and imperfection of earthly processes compared to the perfect, circular motions of the heavens.</note> like inferior things. 

And they say he was nourished by **Thetis**, who is the Sea, because—in animals as in the earth—this heat is nourished by humidity, and that humidity sustains it, whether it is intense or diminished, insofar as the proportioned natural moisture is sufficient or insufficient. They say he is the blacksmith and craftsman of **Jupiter** because he is the minister of so many wondrous operations, specifically Jovial <note>term: Jovial (meaning "of Jupiter"). In Renaissance physiology, the "Jovial" functions are those governed by Jupiter: life-giving, noble, and formative processes within the body.</note> ones, as exist in the human body. 

**Venusian** concupiscence, being thus married and joined with natural heat, falls in love with **Mars**, who is the fervent desire of lasciviousness; for he provides burning libido and lust if it is immoderate. For this reason, they say **Mars** was not born of the seed of **Jupiter**, nor did he participate in any good thing from him, but was born from the striking of **Juno's** womb <note>original: "percussione della Vulva di Iunone"</note>, which signifies the toxicity of the mother’s menses. This is because **Mars**, with his burning changes, causes the power of **Juno's** matter to overcome the reason of **Jupiter**. 

So it is that concupiscent **Venus** is accustomed to falling in love with burning **Mars**. Therefore, the **Astrologers** place a very great friendship between these two planets, and they say that **Venus** corrects all the malice of **Mars** with her benign aspect; and that when lust exceeds through the mixture of them both, the **Sun** (which is clear human reason) reveals them, and thus accuses them to **Vulcan**. Making it known that through that excess, natural heat...

comes to fail. Wherefore he places invisible chains, and shamefully both adulterers find themselves captured. Because as the **natural heat** <term>Natural heat: in Renaissance medicine, the innate warmth of a living being that drives digestion and vitality.</term> fails, the power of **Lust** fails, and excessive desires find themselves bound, without liberty or power, naked of effect, and shamed with penitence. And so shamed, **Vulcan** shows them to the **Gods**. This means he makes the defect of natural heat felt by all human powers, which because of their virtuous operations are called divine, all of which remain defective with the failing of natural heat. And they specify three Gods: **Neptune**, **Mercury**, and **Apollo**, who are three heads of the powers of the human body.

**Neptune** is the **nutritive soul**, with the natural virtues and powers that come from the **Liver**, which are made with an abundance of humidity, over which **Neptune** <note>Neptune, god of the sea, is here associated with the "humid" humors of the liver.</note> presides. **Mercury** is the **sensitive soul**, which contains sense, motion, and cogitation, which proceed from the **Brain**, which are proper to **Mercury**. **Apollo** is the **vital pulsing soul**, which provides the spirits and the natural heat through the artery, which has its origin in the **Heart**; for, as I told you above, the **Heart** in the human body is like **Apollo** in the world. 

So, whoever follows the path of excessive lust brings harm and shame to the **Heart** and its virtues, to the **Brain** and its virtues, and to the **Liver** and its virtues; none of these are sufficient to appease **Vulcan**, nor to remedy his defect, except **Neptune**. This is the nutritive virtue, which, with its abundant humidity, can recover the consumed natural heat and restore the power of lust to liberty. They say that **Venus** had a very great hatred for the progeny of the **Sun** <note>Apollo/The Sun represents Reason and Prudence.</note>, and that she caused his daughters to commit adultery, converting them to her own nature. And for this reason, they depict him as a small child, naked, blind, with wings, and shooting arrows. They depict him as a child because love always grows and is unbridled, as children are. They depict him naked because he cannot be covered or disguised. Blind, because he cannot see any reason to the contrary, as passion blinds <note>Original: "pacieca" (acceca).</note> him. <meta>This page continues Philo’s explanation of the sensitive soul and its associated deity, Mercury. The previous page established that the brain and its functions (senses and thought) are governed by Mercury, following the discussion of the liver (Neptune) and the heart (Apollo).</meta>

<sig>E ij</sig>

...[the sensitive soul remains obsessed] with the thought, and stays always with the loved person and lives in them. The arrows are those with which he <note>Referring to Cupid, or Love personified.</note> pierces the hearts of lovers; these arrows make narrow, deep, and incurable wounds, which most often arise from corresponding rays from the eyes of the lovers, which act in the manner of arrows.

SOPHIA. Tell me also: how did Venus give birth to Hermaphroditus by Mercury?

PHILO. You must know that the poets say Mercury was born of **Caelus** <note>Sky</note> and **Dies** <note>Day</note>, and that he is the brother of **Venus**; and according to others, they make him the son of **Jupiter** and nursed by **Juno**. This Mercury they say is the God of Eloquence, God of the Sciences—especially Mathematics, Arithmetic, Geometry, Music, and Astrology—God of Medicine, God of Markets, God of Thieves, Messenger of Jupiter, and Interpreter of the Gods. His signs are a rod entwined by a serpent <note>The caduceus, a symbol of heralds and physicians.</note>, and from these concepts many fables are told about him. But in effect, the planet Mercury influences these types of things in the nature of man, according to its disposition.

Hence, if he is found strong and in good aspect <term>Aspect: the angular distance between planets in astrology, which determines whether their influence is harmonious or difficult.</term>, he gives elegant eloquence, sweet speech, learning, and genius in the mathematical sciences. And with the aspect of **Jupiter**, he makes philosophers and theologians. And with a good aspect of **Mars**, he makes true physicians. And with a bad aspect, he makes thieves, especially when he is combust <term>Combust: an astrological condition where a planet is so close to the Sun that its power is "burned up" or hidden.</term> by the Sun. From this comes the fable that he stole the cattle of **Apollo**; and they say that he fathered the thief **Autolycus** by **Chione** <note>The transcription says "Lichione," but this refers to the mythological figure Chione.</note>. 

And with **Venus**, he makes poets, musicians, and versifiers. And with the **Moon**, he makes merchants and traders. And with **Saturn**, he gives the deepest knowledge and divination of future things. This is because he is, by his own nature, mutable into the nature of the planet with which he mixes; for when mixing with a masculine planet, he is male, and with a feminine one, he is female.

And among men, many were called "Mercuries," especially certain wise men of Egypt and physicians who shared in Mercurial virtues. And because Mercury is a bright planet, they make him the son of Sky and Day, because he participates in the celestial substance and diurnal light, since the light of all planets comes from the Sun, which makes the day. He is the brother of **Venus** because the planets are conjoined; these two planets are conjoined, and each of them revolves through its orb in almost the same time—that is, in one year—and they always follow the Sun without straying too far from it, and for this reason, they say they are siblings. 

Others place Mercury as the son of **Jupiter** because of his divine wisdom and virtue. And they say he was nursed by **Juno**, because human wisdom proceeds from divinity and is sustained by material writings, which are signified by **Juno**. They call him the Messenger of **Jupiter** because he announces and predicts future things that the almighty God wants...

<meta>This page continues Philo’s explanation of the sensitive soul and the symbolic roles of the gods in human physiology. Having previously discussed Mercury’s association with the brain, he now turns to the myth of Vulcan catching Venus and Mars in a net to explain the physical decline of desire and the interaction between the body's vital organs.</meta>

fails. Hence he places invisible chains, and shamefully both the adulterers <note>Venus (representing lust) and Mars (representing fervor or heat).</note> are found caught. For as the **natural heat** <term>Natural heat: In Renaissance medicine, this was the innate warmth that fueled life and digestion, thought to be centered in the heart.</term> fails, the power of **libido** <note>Lust or sexual desire.</note> also fails, and excessive desires are found bound—without liberty or power, stripped of effect, and shamed by repentance. And so, in their shame, **Vulcan** shows them to the Gods. This means that he makes the defect of natural heat felt by all human powers (which, for their virtuous operations, are called "divine"), all of which remain defective when natural heat fails.

And they specify three Gods: **Neptune**, **Mercury**, and **Apollo**, who are the three heads of the powers of the human body. 

*   **Neptune** is the **nutritive soul** <note>The "vegetative" part of the soul responsible for growth and nutrition.</note>, with the natural virtues and powers that come from the **Liver**; these are created with an abundance of moisture, over which Neptune presides. 
*   **Mercury** is the **sensitive soul**, which contains sense, motion, and thought, proceeding from the **Brain**, which is the proper domain of Mercury. 
*   **Apollo** is the **vital, pulsing soul**, which provides the spirits and natural heat through the arteries; this has its origin in the **Heart**, because, as I told you above, the heart in the human body is like Apollo in the world. 

So, if damage and shame follow from excessive libido to the Heart and its virtues, to the Brain and its virtues, or to the Liver and its virtues, none of them is sufficient to appease Vulcan or to remedy his defect, except Neptune. For it is the nutritive virtue which, with its abundant moisture, can recover the consumed natural heat and restore the power of libido to its liberty.

They say that **Venus** held a very great hatred for the progeny of the Sun <note>Apollo, representing Reason or Light.</note>, and that she caused his daughters to commit adultery, converting them to her nature. This is because **Love** is the enemy of **Reason**, and **Lust** is the opposite of **Prudence**; not only does it not obey her, but it even transgresses and "adulterates" all her counsels and judgments, converting them to its own inclination, judging that inclination and its effects to be good and feasible, and executing them with the greatest diligence.

SOPHIA. I have heard enough regarding Mars and Venus, and for this reason the Poets must say that Cupid was born from these two lovers.

PHILO. So it is; for the true **Cupid**, who is the passion of love and complete concupiscence, is made from the lasciviousness of Venus and the fervor of Mars. And for this reason they paint him as a naked, blind boy with wings and arrows.

*   They paint him as a **boy**, because love always grows and is unrestrained, just like children.
*   They paint him **naked**, because love cannot be covered or disguised.
*   **Blind**, because it cannot see any reason to the contrary, as passion blinds it.
*   They paint him **winged**, because he is most swift, so that the mind does not...

<summary>Philo explains the myth of Vulcan catching Mars and Venus as an allegory for the loss of physical heat and libido. He maps the gods Neptune, Mercury, and Apollo to the liver, brain, and heart, respectively, while detailing the symbolic iconography of Cupid.</summary>

...there with the thought, and always stays with the beloved person, and lives in them. The arrows are those with which he pierces the hearts of lovers; these arrows cause wounds that are narrow, deep, and incurable, which most often come from the corresponding rays of the eyes of lovers, which are like arrows.

**SO.** Tell me also how **Venus** <note>The goddess of love and the planet governing desire.</note> gave birth to **Hermaphroditus** by **Mercury**.

**PH.** You must know that the Poets say that **Mercury** was born of **Heaven** and of **Day** <note>original: "Dia." Referring to the personification of daylight.</note>, and that he is the brother of Venus; according to others, they make him the son of **Jove** <note>Jupiter, the king of the gods.</note>, and nursed by **Juno**. This Mercury they say is the God of Eloquence, the God of Sciences—especially **Mathematics**, **Arithmetic**, **Geometry**, **Music**, and **Astrology**—the God of **Medicine**, the God of **Merchants**, the God of **Thieves**, the Messenger of Jove, and the Interpreter of the Gods. His insignia is a rod surrounded by a serpent <note>The caduceus, a symbol of heralds and later of medicine.</note>. From these intentions many fables are told of him, but in effect, the planet Mercury influences these types of things according to its disposition in the nature of man.

Hence, if he is found there "strong" and with a "good aspect" <term>Aspect: In astrology, the specific angle between two planets that determines whether their influence is harmonious or challenging.</term>, he grants elegant eloquence, sweet speech, learning, and ingenuity in the mathematical sciences. And with the aspect of Jove, he creates Philosophers and Theologians. And with a good aspect of **Mars**, he creates true Doctors. And with a "bad aspect," he creates Thieves, especially when he is **combust** by the Sun <term>Combust: An astrological condition where a planet is so close to the Sun that its own power is "burned up" or weakened.</term>. From this comes the fable that he stole the Cattle of **Apollo**; and they say he sired, by **Lycaon**, **Autolycus** the thief. And with Venus, he creates Poets, Music, and Versifiers. And with the **Moon**, he creates Merchants and traders. 

And with **Saturn**, he gives the most profound science and divination of future things. Because he is, by his very nature, mutable into the nature of the planet with which he mixes; when mixing with a masculine planet, he is male, and with a feminine one, he is female. And among men, many were called "Mercuries," especially certain sages of Egypt and Doctors who shared in these Mercurial virtues. 

And because Mercury is a bright planet, they make him the son of Heaven and of Day, because he participates in the celestial substance and daylight, since the light of all planets comes from the Sun, which creates the day. He is the brother of Venus because the planets are conjoined, and these two planets are conjoined, and each of them completes its **Orb** <note>Its orbital path or sphere.</note> in nearly the same time—that is, in one year—and they always stay near the Sun without straying too far from him; for this reason, they say they are siblings. Others place Mercury as the son of Jove because of his divine wisdom and virtue. And they say he was nursed by Juno because human wisdom appears from divinity and is sustained in material writings—signified by Juno <note>Juno often represents the lower, material air or the physical world in Neoplatonic allegory.</note>. They call him the Messenger of Jove because he announces and predicts the future things that the almighty God wills...

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Sophia and Philo concerning the nature of Mercury. Philo is moving from a list of the god's titles to an allegorical interpretation of his symbols, specifically the caduceus (his rod).</meta>

...do, and for this, and for his eloquence, they call him the interpreter of the Gods. His rod is the rectitude of ingenuity <note>Or "uprightness of mind," suggesting a direct and honest intellectual path.</note> that is found in the sciences, and the Serpent that surrounds it is the subtle discourse that winds around that upright ingenuity; or rather, the rod is the speculative intellect <term>Speculative Intellect: The part of the mind concerned with pure knowledge and contemplation of truth for its own sake.</term> of science, and the Serpent is the active intellect and prudence <note>Prudence here refers to "practical wisdom" or the ability to navigate moral choices.</note> regarding moral virtues; for the Serpent, by its sagacity, is a sign of prudence, and the rod, by its rectitude and firmness, is a sign of science.

**SOPHIA.** What of that which they say, that the rod was given to him by **Apollo**?

**PHILO.** The fable is that **Mercury** stole the cows of Apollo, and being seen by **Battus**, he gave him a cow so that he would keep silent; but doubting him, Mercury wanted to test the man's faith, and so he transformed himself into the shape of another. He came to Battus, promising him an ox if he would reveal who had stolen the cows. This Battus told him everything, and so Mercury, fearing Apollo, turned him into stone <note>A classic myth from Ovid’s *Metamorphoses* illustrating the punishment for double-crossing or greed.</note>. Finally, the truth having been manifested to Apollo through his divinity, he took up his bow to shoot Mercury; but Mercury, making himself invisible, could not be hit. Later they reached an agreement, and Mercury presented Apollo with the **Lyre** <note>original: "Cetara." A stringed instrument associated with harmony and the arts.</note>, and Apollo gave him the rod. Others say that Mercury, having foreseen the fury of Apollo, secretly emptied the quiver of Apollo’s arrows. Seeing this, Apollo (even though he was angry) laughed at Mercury’s cunning, and forgave him and gave him the rod, and received from him the Lyre.

**SOPHIA.** What does such a fable mean to signify?

**PHILO.** It signifies that **Mercurials** <note>People born under the astrological influence of the planet Mercury, traditionally thought to be quick-witted but often of low social standing.</note> are poor, but they are cunning enough to expertly acquire abundance and riches from Kings and other great masters through deception; for they are accustomed to being administrators and Royal secretaries because of the Mercurial aptitude they possess. This is what it means when it says that Mercury stole the cows of Apollo: for Apollo signifies powerful lords, and the cows are their riches and their abundance. And when princes are angry against them for their thefts, they free themselves from that anger with Mercurial cunning, removing the causes from which punishment could come; by mitigating the fury of the lords, they remain in their favor. Furthermore, because Mercurials are of low status, they are not harmed by the furies of great masters, because they offer no resistance to them. And thus Mercury is the smallest of all the other planets, whereby the solar rays and their combustion <term>Combustion: An astrological term for when a planet is so close to the Sun that its own influence is supposedly "burnt up" or hidden.</term> harm him less than any other planet. Once Mercury and Apollo are reconciled together, Mercury gives Apollo the Lyre, and Apollo gives him the Rod. This means...

This is because **Mercury** is entirely intellectual, and **Venus** is entirely corporeal; therefore, when these two natures are mixed, they create a counterfeit and unnatural lust.

**SOPHIA.** Of the love affairs, marriages, and lineages of the celestial Gods and of their natures, you have told me plenty—as much regarding the universal father **Demogorgon** as the celestial fathers **Aether** and **Coelius**, and of the planets that successively proceed from them: namely **Saturn**, **Jupiter**, **Mars**, **Venus**, and **Mercury**. Nothing remains for me to know except for the children of **Latona** and **Jove**—that is, **Apollo** and **Diana**. Although I have no reason to ask about **Diana**, she having always been, as they say, a virgin, I only wish to know about the love of **Apollo** for **Daphne**, who they say was turned into a **Laurel** while fleeing from him.

**PHILO.** Regarding the generation of **Diana** and **Apollo**, you have already heard it all. They make **Diana** a virgin because the excessive coldness of the **Moon** takes away the moisture and the heat of lust from those in whose nativity she holds dominion. They call her the Goddess of mountains and forests because she has great power in the...

<meta>This page continues Philo’s discourse on the allegorical meanings of the gods. Having finished discussing Mercury and the Hermaphrodite, he now moves on to the moon (Diana) and the sun (Apollo), explaining their attributes through the lenses of nature, medicine, and music.</meta>

the germination of grasses and trees, with which she feeds all wild animals; and they call her a huntress because her light at night aids hunters, and they also call her the guardian of the ways because, with her nocturnal light, she makes the paths safer for travelers. They say she carries a bow and arrows because her rays are often harmful to animals, especially when entering through narrow openings like arrows. They assign her a chariot drawn by **white deer** to signify, by their speed, that her motion is faster than that of any other celestial orb—since she finishes her circuit in a single month—and whiteness is her proper color. She is called **Luna** because, when she is new, she illuminates the beginning of the night; and she is called **Diana** because, when she is old <note>Philo refers to the waning moon appearing in the pre-dawn sky.</note>, she precedes the day, illuminating the morning before the rising of the **Sun**, and also because she often appears during the day.

**SOPHIA.** I have heard enough of **Diana**; tell me of **Apollo**, and of his falling in love, for these loves of the celestial Gods are all I lack.

**PHILO.** **Apollo**, according to the poets, is the God of Wisdom and of Medicine; he has the **Lyre** <note>original: "Cetara."</note> which **Mercury** gave him, because he presides over the **Muses**; they attribute to him the **Laurel** and the **Horn**, and say that he carries a bow and arrows.

**SOPHIA.** Tell me the meaning of this.

**PHILO.** He is the God of Wisdom because he especially rules the heart and illuminates the <term>spirits</term> <note>In Renaissance physiology, "spirits" (spiriti) were subtle substances that carried life, sensation, and thought through the body.</note>, which are the origin of human knowledge and wisdom; also because by his light, sensible things are seen and discerned, from which knowledge and wisdom are derived. He is the God of Medicine because the virtue of the heart, and the natural heat that depends on it throughout the whole body, preserves health and heals diseases; also because the tempered heat of the **Sun** in the **Spring** heals the long illnesses remaining from **Winter** and **Autumn**, in which times, being cold, the heat of the **Sun** is weak and diminished, and therefore many illnesses are caused then which are healed by the renewal of the heat in **Spring**. He is given the **Lyre**, and they say he is the God of Music, because he creates the harmony of the pulse which proceeds from the spirits of the heart throughout the entire human body—a harmony which sensible physicians recognize by touch—and also because of the celestial harmony made from the diversity of movements of all the orbs, which (as I have told you) **Pythagoras** maintains also consists of a concordance of voices. The **Sun** (being the largest, the most lucid, and the principal among all the other planets, like a captain over them all) is that which generates all harmony, and

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Sophia and Philo regarding the mythological attributes of the sun god, Apollo, transitioning from his role in celestial harmony to his associations with prophecy and his pursuit of the nymph Daphne.</meta>

For this reason, they attribute the **Lyre** <note>original: "Cetara"</note> to him, and say he received it from **Mercury**, because **Mercury** provides concordance and harmonic proportion; but the **Sun**, as the principal, is the master of celestial **Music**, and not without reason, since its motion is more orderly than that of any of the others. It always travels through the middle of the **Zodiac** without departing from it, always straight in its motion, whereby it is the measure of the motions of the others, just as it is that which gives light to all the others. And this is what they say about him being the president of the **Muses**, who are nine, meaning the nine celestial **Orbs** <note>In the Renaissance understanding of the cosmos, these included the seven planets, the fixed stars, and the *Primum Mobile* or "First Mover."</note> that create harmony, of which he is the one who forms the universal concordance.

His arrows are the solar rays, which many times cause harm through excessive heat, or through the toxicity of the air, which is why they make him the author of the **Plague**. Among the trees, they appropriate the **Laurel** to him, for being the **God of Wisdom**. He influences **Divination**, wherefore they say that once he had killed **Python**, he began to give oracles in **Delos**. And of the **Laurel** it is written that when a man sleeps with his head surrounded by its leaves, he dreams true things, and his dreams partake in divination. And also for this reason they appropriate the **Raven** <note>original: "Corbo"</note> to him, because they say the **Raven** has sixty-four different voices, from which auguries, auspices, and divinations are taken more than from any other animal.

**SOPHIA.** This is enough for me concerning the nature and condition of **Apollo**; tell me that which pertains to his falling in love with **Daphne**.

**PHILO.** The poem is that while **Apollo** was boasting, in the presence of **Cupid**, of the power of his bow and his arrows, with which he had killed the most venomous serpent **Python**, he seemed almost not to value the strength of **Cupid's** bow and arrows, but to despise them as childish weapons, unsuitable for such terrible blows. Whereat **Cupid**, becoming indignant, struck **Apollo** with a golden arrow, and **Daphne**, daughter of the river **Peneus**, with one of lead. Thus he caused **Apollo** to love the virgin **Daphne**, and he followed her as one follows gold; and in **Daphne** he made love for **Apollo** grow heavy, as lead is heavy, so that she fled from him continually. And **Daphne**, seeing herself so pursued and almost caught by **Apollo**, asked for help from her father **Peneus** and the other rivers, who, to save her from him, converted her into a **Laurel**. And **Apollo**, finding her thus turned into a **Laurel**, still embraced her, and she trembled with fear; finally, **Apollo** took some of her leaves and adorned his **Lyre** and his **Quiver** with them, and appropriated the **Laurel** to himself as his own tree, whereat **Daphne** remained content with him.

**SOPHIA.** Tell me the allegory.

**PHILO.** They wish to show how much...

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Philo and Sophia, moving from Apollo’s general attributes (the lyre, divination, and the laurel) to a deeper philosophical and allegorical interpretation of his "bow" and "arrows." It connects the Greek myth of Python with the biblical narrative of the Great Flood.</meta>

Great and universal is the power of love, even in the most exalted and powerful God of all the celestials, which is the Sun. Whence they elegantly imagine <note>original: "fingano"</note> that he boasted how, with his bow and his arrows (which are his burning rays), he killed the horrible serpent **Python**, who was destroying everything. This thing, as I have told you, signifies the wateriness of the flood which remained scattered over the whole earth and prohibited the <term>generation</term> <note>The biological process of reproduction and growth.</note> and nutrition of men and all other terrestrial animals; which wateriness the Sun, with his burning and arrow-like rays, dried up and gave life to those who live upon the earth.

And so that you may know, O <term>Sophia</term> <note>Greek: Σοφία; meaning "Wisdom."</note>, what the Bow of Apollo is precisely—beyond his course and the solar circumference with which he removed the damage of the flood and assured us against the cruel **Python**—I tell you that it is that true bow of diverse colors which appears in the air opposite the Sun when the weather is humid and rainy. The Greeks call this Bow <term>Iris</term> <note>The rainbow, personified as a messenger goddess in Greek mythology.</note>, and they mean that which the sacred scripture narrates in <term>Genesis</term>. After the flood had passed, with only **Noah** remaining among men—a just man with his three sons, who saved himself in a floating Ark with a male and a female of every species of terrestrial animal—God assured him that the flood would proceed no further. He gave him as a sign that bow, **Iris**, which is generated in the clouds after it has rained, which provides assurance that no flood can happen again.

And since this Bow is formed by the radiation of the Sun’s circumference into the humid and thick clouds, it causes the difference in their thickness to show a diversity of colors according to the manner in which the clouds receive the light. It follows that the Bow of the Sun is that which creates, by God’s command, the stability and security of there never being another flood.

**SO.** In what way does the Sun, with his Bow, give us such security?

**PH.** The Sun does not impress the Bow when the air is thin and clear, but when it is thick and humid. If this air were of a dense thickness sufficient to cause a flood through a multitude of rains, it would not be capable of receiving the light's impression; therefore, the Bow assures us that the clouds do not have the thickness to be able to cause a flood. This is the stability and security that the Bow gives against there being a flood. The cause of this is the power of the Sun, which purifies the clouds to such an extent and thins them in such a way that, by imprinting its circumference upon them, it makes them fit for light and insufficient to create a flood. Whence, with effective reason and prudence, they have

<summary>Philo explains the myth of Apollo and Python as an allegory for the Sun drying the Earth after the Great Flood, linking the Greek "Iris" to the biblical rainbow given to Noah as a physical sign that the clouds are too thin to cause another universal deluge.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the allegorical interpretation of Apollo. Having previously discussed the Sun’s role in drying the post-flood world (the killing of Python), the text now moves to the famous myth of Apollo and Daphne, interpreting it as a natural process of evaporation, vegetation, and the relationship between celestial heat and terrestrial moisture.</meta>

...it is said that Apollo killed the serpent **Python** with his bow and arrows. For which deed, Apollo being proud and haughty, as is the nature of the **Sun** <note>The author equates the god Apollo with the physical Sun and its astrological properties.</note>, he could not, however, free himself from the bow of **Cupid** <note>original: "Cupidine"</note>; for love not only compels the lower to love the higher, but also draws the higher to love the lower. For this reason, Apollo loved **Daphne**, daughter of the river **Peneus**.

She is the **humidity** of the earth that comes from the rivers which pass through it. This humidity loves the Sun; as he sends his burning rays into it, he seeks to attract it to himself, exhaling it into vapors.

And one could say that the purpose of such exhalation was the nourishment of the **Celestials** <note>The "celestials" refers to the stars and planets, which ancient and Renaissance thinkers sometimes believed were "fed" by vapors rising from the Earth.</note>, because poets hold that they are nourished by the vapors that ascend from the humidity of the earth's globe. But since this is also metaphorical, it is understood that the Sun and the **Planets** are maintained in their proper office—which is to govern and sustain the lower world and, consequently, the whole of the universe—by means of the exhalation of humid vapors. Therefore, he [the Sun] loves humidity to convert it to himself for his needs.

But she [the humidity] flees from the Sun, because all things flee from what consumes them. Furthermore, the solar rays cause the humidity to penetrate through the pores of the earth and make it flee from the surface; thus the Sun dissolves it. When it is already inside the earth and can no longer flee from the Sun, it converts into trees and plants, with the help and influence of the celestial Gods, the generators of things, and with the help of the rivers, which restore it and succor it from the Sun's persecution and grasp.

They say, according to the fable, that she was converted into a **Laurel** <note>original: "Lauro"; the Greek myth explains the origin of the laurel tree through Daphne's transformation to escape Apollo.</note>, because the Laurel, being an excellent and long-lasting tree, is always green, fragrant, and warm in its nature; the mixing of solar rays with terrestrial moisture is manifested more in it than in any other tree.

They say she was the daughter of the river **Peneus**, because the land where the said Peneus passes generates many Laurels. They say that Apollo adorned his **Lyre** <note>original: "Cetara"</note> and his **Quiver** <note>original: "Faretra"</note> with those leaves.

By this, they mean to signify that the sweet and clear **Poets**, who play the Lyre of Apollo, and the victorious and famous **Captains**, together with the reigning **Emperors**, signify, and in effect are the...

<summary>This page provides a naturalistic allegory of the myth of Apollo and Daphne, characterizing Daphne as terrestrial humidity that "flees" the Sun’s heat to become vegetation, specifically the laurel tree. It explains why the laurel is sacred to poets, captains, and emperors.</summary>
<keywords>Apollo, Daphne, Peneus, Laurel, allegory, evaporation, solar heat, humidity, Cupid, Lyre, Quiver</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the allegorical analysis of the myth of Apollo and Daphne. The previous page established that Apollo represents the Sun and Daphne represents terrestrial humidity. This page transitions from the symbolic meaning of the laurel tree to a technical explanation of the celestial spheres and the zodiac signs.</meta>

**Quiver of the Sun**, which properly gives forth clear fame, powerful victories, and exalted triumphs; these are the only things that are usually crowned with **Laurel**, as a sign of eternal honor and glorious fame. Just as the Laurel lasts a long time, so the name of the wise and the victorious is immortal; and just as the Laurel is always green, so their fame is always young and never grows old or dry. Furthermore, just as the Laurel is hot and fragrant, so the fervent spirits of these men give off the sweetest scent in distant places, from one part of the world to the other.

For this reason, this tree is called Laurel <note>original: "Lauro"</note> because it is among the other trees as **Gold** <note>original: "Oro"</note> is among the other metals. Also, because it is written that the ancients called it "Praise" <note>original: "Laudo"</note> for its praises, and because those who were worthy of eternal praise were crowned with its leaves. For this reason, this is the tree that is attributed to the **Sun**; and they say that heaven's lightning <note>original: "saetta del cielo"</note> cannot strike it, because the fame of virtue cannot be undone by time, nor even by the movements and celestial changes which strike every other thing in this lower world with aging, corruption, and oblivion.

**SO.** <note>Likely "Sophia" or "Wisdom," the student in this dialogue.</note> I am satisfied by what you have said regarding the loves of all the celestial Gods—both of the **Orbs** <note>The concentric spheres thought to carry the planets around the Earth.</note> and the seven **Planets**—as well as the love affairs of the other earthly and human Gods. I do not wish for you to take any further trouble on that, as it does not matter much to wisdom. But I would very much like you to declare to me, without fables or fictions, what the wise **Astrologers** hold regarding the loves and hates that the celestial bodies and the planets have, particularly for one another.

**PH.** <note>Likely "Philo" or "The Philosopher," the teacher in this dialogue.</note> Briefly I will tell you part of what you ask, for the whole would be far too long. The celestial Orbs (which Astrologers have been able to recognize) are nine. The seven closest to us are the Orbs of the seven **erratic planets** <note>The "wandering stars": Moon, Mercury, Venus, Sun, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn.</note>. Of the other two higher ones, there is the **Eighth**, which is where the great multitude of the stars that are seen remain fixed. The **Ninth** and last is the **Diurnal** <note>The "Primum Mobile" or first moved sphere, which completes a rotation every 24 hours.</note>, which in one day and one night—that is, in twenty-four hours—turns its entire circuit, and in this span of time it turns all the other celestial bodies with it. The circuit of these higher celestial Orbs is divided into a measurement of 360 degrees, divided into 12 signs of 30 degrees each. This circuit is called the **Zodiac**, which means "circle of animals," because those 12 signs are represented by animals:

->**The Ram** <note>original: "Aries"</note> / **The Bull** <note>original: "Tauro"</note> / **The Twins** <note>original: "Gemini"</note> / **The Crab** <note>original: "Cancer"</note> / **The Lion** <note>original: "Leo"</note> / **The Virgin** <note>original: "Virgo"</note> / **The Scales** <note>original: "Libra"</note> / **The Scorpion** <note>original: "Scorpio"</note> / **The Archer** <note>original: "Sagittario"</note> / **The Goat** <note>original: "Capricorno"</note> / **The Water-Bearer** <note>original: "Aquario"</note> / & **The Fish** <note>original: "Pesce"</note><-

of which three are of the nature of fire—hot and dry—namely: **The Ram**, **The Lion**, and **The Archer**.

<meta>This page continues the technical transition mentioned at the end of the previous page. ✓ . 3 . are of the nature of earth: cold and dry, namely Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn. 3 are of the nature of air: hot and moist, namely Gemini, Libra, and Aquarius. And 3 are of the nature of water: cold and moist, namely Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces. These signs have among them friendship and hatred; because every three signs share the same <term>complexion (the balance of qualities like hot, cold, moist, and dry)</term>, they divide the sky into thirds and are separated by only 120 degrees. For this reason, they are complete friends, such as Aries with Leo and Sagittarius; Taurus with Virgo and Capricorn; Gemini with Libra and Aquarius; and Cancer with Scorpio and Pisces. The harmony of the <term>trine aspect (an astrological angle of 120°)</term> combined with the same elemental nature brings them together in perfect friendship.

And those signs that divide the Zodiac by six (which are 60 degrees apart) have half-friendship, which is to say imperfect, such as Aries with Gemini, Gemini with Leo, Leo with Libra, Libra with Sagittarius, Sagittarius with Aquarius, and Aquarius with Aries. These (beyond the harmony of the <term>sextile aspect (an astrological angle of 60°)</term>) are compatible because they are all masculine and all share the same active quality—that is, they are hot, whether through the dryness of the fiery nature or the moisture of the airy nature. This is because, in effect, fire and air have a moderate compatibility and friendship between them. 

Even though they are different elements, this same compatibility exists between the 6 other signs of earthy and watery nature, as they too are moderately compatible: namely Taurus with Cancer, Cancer with Virgo, Virgo with Scorpio, Scorpio with Capricorn, Capricorn with Pisces, and Pisces with Taurus. All of these have a sextile aspect of 60 degrees distance and are feminine, sharing the same active quality—which is cold—although they differ in their passive quality, from dry to moist, just as earth differs from water. Thus, their friendship is halfway and imperfect. 

Nevertheless, if the signs are opposite in the Zodiac at the greatest possible distance (that is, 180 degrees), they have complete enmity between them. This is because the position of one is opposite and totally contrary to the other; when one ascends, the other descends, and when one is above the earth, the other is below. And although they always share the same active quality—either both hot or both cold—they are always contrary in their passive quality. If one is moist, the other is dry. This, added to the opposite distance and aspect, makes them mortal enemies, such as Aries with Libra, Taurus with Scorpio, Gemini with Sagittarius, Cancer with Capricorn, and Leo with Aquarius,

& Virgo with Pisces; and when they are distant by a quarter of the Zodiac, which is 90 degrees, they are half-enemies. This is both because the distance is half of an opposition and because their natures are always contrary in both qualities—active and passive. <note>In traditional physics and astrology, the "active" qualities are hot and cold, while the "passive" qualities are moist and dry.</note> For if one is fiery (hot and dry), the other is watery (cold and moist); and if one is an airy sign (hot and moist), the other is earthy (cold and dry). Examples include Aries with Cancer, Leo with Scorpio, and Sagittarius with Pisces—where one is fiery and the other watery. Similarly, Gemini with Virgo, Libra with Capricorn, and Aquarius with Taurus—where one is airy and the other earthy. Or, they are at least contrary in their active quality, where one is hot and the other is cold, such as Taurus with Leo, Virgo with Sagittarius, and Capricorn with Aries; and likewise Cancer with Libra, Scorpio with Aquarius, and Pisces with Gemini. All of these have a contrariety of active qualities between them, with a **square aspect** of half-enmity.

**SO.** <note>Likely "Socrates" or "Scholar," representing the student in this dialogue.</note> I have understood well how perfect and imperfect love and hatred are found among the 12 signs of heaven. I would now like you to tell me if these are also found among the 7 planets.

**PH.** <note>Likely "Philosopher" or "Philo," the teacher.</note> The planets love one another when they look upon each other with a benign aspect, such as the **trine**, a distance of 120 degrees, which is the aspect of perfect love. Or they may share a **sextile** aspect, which is half that distance—60 degrees from one to the other—which is the aspect of slow love and half-friendship. But they create enmity and hate one another when they look upon each other in the **opposite aspect**, at the greatest distance possible in the sky: 180 degrees. This is the aspect of total hatred, perfect enmity, and total opposition. Furthermore, when they look at each other from a **square aspect**, which is half that distance (90 degrees from one to the other), it is an aspect of half-enmity and slow hatred.

**SO.** You have spoken of the aspects—that the trine and sextile grant love, and the opposite and square grant hatred. Tell me, when they are in <term>conjunction (when two planets occupy the same degree of the Zodiac)</term>, do they act in love or in dislike?

**PH.** The conjunction of two planets is either loving or hateful according to the nature of the two joined together. If two benign planets—called <term>fortunes (planets like Jupiter and Venus believed to have a positive influence)</term>—are joined, namely **Jupiter** and **Venus**, they offer love and benevolence to one another. And if the **Moon** joins with either of them, it makes for a happy and loving conjunction. If the **Sun** joins with them, it makes for a troublesome and inimical conjunction, because it makes them <term>combust (an astrological condition where a planet is so close to the Sun that its power is "burned up" or weakened)</term> and of little value; although the Sun itself is good in some respects, it is not so in this case due to their combustion. Mercury with Jupiter makes a conjunction...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how planets interact with one another. ...a happy and amicable relationship; and with Venus, it makes it amorous, though not very upright. With the Moon, it has a mediocre friendship, but with the Sun, it is **combust** <note>original: *combusto*; in astrology, a planet is "combust" when it is so close to the Sun that its own influence is "burnt up" or hidden by the Sun's power</note>, and its conjunction is not very friendly—except, however, if they were perfectly united. In that case, bodily, it would make for an excellent and most amorous conjunction, and through it, the vigor of the Sun increases, as if there were two Suns in the sky.

The conjunction of the Sun with the Moon is very hateful, although when they are entirely and bodily united, some astrologers consider it friendly, especially for secret matters. But the conjunction of either of the two **malefic** <note>original: *pianeti infortuny*; planets traditionally associated with difficulty or misfortune, namely Saturn and Mars</note> planets, Saturn and Mars, with any other planet is hateful, except for the conjunction of Mars with Venus, which creates amorous lust and excess.

The conjunction of Saturn with Jupiter is amorous toward Saturn and hateful toward Jupiter; but their conjunction with the Sun, just as it is unfriendly to the Sun, is also harmful to them, because the Sun burns them and weakens their power even in doing evil. With Mercury and the Moon, they have a terrible conjunction, which is not useful even to themselves.

**SO.** <note>The student/scholar in the dialogue.</note> Just as conjunctions differ in good and evil according to the nature of the joined planets, are the benevolent aspects between them also so varied? Or indeed the malevolent ones, according to the natures of the two "looking" at each other?

**PH.** <note>The philosopher/teacher.</note> Benevolent aspects vary, and so do malevolent ones, more or less according to the planets that are looking. For when the two **benefics** <note>original: *fortune*; planets associated with luck and growth</note>, Jupiter and Venus, look at each other with a **trine aspect** <note>a 120-degree angle</note> or a **sextile** <note>a 60-degree angle</note>, it is an excellent aspect. If it is an **opposition** <note>180 degrees</note> or a **square** <note>90 degrees</note>, they look at each other with enmity; they cannot inflict any evil, but they provide little good, and only with difficulty.

And so, when either of them looks at the Moon, Mercury, or the Sun with an amorous aspect, it signifies happiness of fortune in its nature; and if they look at each other with an unfriendly aspect, they signify little good, and that which is obtained is through difficulty. But if these two benefics look at the two malefics—that is, Saturn and Mars—with a good aspect, they give mediocre good, but with some fear and displeasure. If they look at them with a bad aspect, they give evil under the guise of good, except for Mars with Venus, who have such a good temperament together that when they have a good aspect between them, they are very favorable, especially in amorous matters.

Furthermore, Jupiter with Saturn, looking at each other with a good aspect, produces divine, high, and good things, far removed from sensuality. Additionally, a fortunate Jupiter corrects the hardness of Saturn. And Venus, when well-placed, corrects the cruelty and wickedness of Mars. Mercury with a good aspect to Mars or Saturn does little good; and with a bad aspect, he does great evil.

**SO.** In what order are these twelve signs distributed as houses for the seven planets?

**PH.** The Sun and the Moon each have one house in the heavens: that of the Sun is Leo, and that of the Moon is Cancer. The other five planets have two houses each: **Saturn** has Capricorn and Aquarius; **Jupiter** <note>original: *Iuppiter*.</note> has Sagittarius and Pisces; **Mars** has Aries and Scorpio; **Venus** has Taurus and Libra; and **Mercury** has Gemini and Virgo.

**SO.** Tell me if there is any reason assigned to the order of these partitions.

These are the two signs where, when the Sun is in them (which is from mid-December until mid-February), the weather is the coldest and most stormy of the entire year, which are things belonging to the nature of Saturn. 

Jupiter, being the second planet near Saturn, has its two houses in the Zodiac near those of Saturn: Sagittarius before Capricorn, and Pisces after Aquarius. 

Mars, which is the third planet near Jupiter <note>original: *Gioue*.</note>, has its two houses near his: Scorpio before Sagittarius, and Aries after Pisces. 

Venus, which according to the ancients is the fourth planet near Mars, has its houses near those: that is, Libra before Scorpio, and Taurus after Aries. 

Mercury, which is the fifth planet near Venus according to the ancients, has its houses near those: that is, Virgo before Libra, and Gemini after Taurus.

**SO.** I like this order, and it conforms to the position of the planets according to the ancients, who placed the Sun below Venus and Mercury; but according to modern astronomers <note>The text refers to the shifting understanding of the geocentric model, specifically the order of the spheres.</note>, who place it near Mars and above Venus, this order would not seem right or reasonable.

**SO.** You place the Moon before the Sun, then? 

**PH.** Do not be surprised by that, for in the sacred creation of the world, Night is placed before Day; and as I told you, according to the Poets, Diana was the Moon at the birth of **Apollo** <note>The Greek and Roman god of the Sun.</note>, so it is rightly ordered that Cancer, the house of the Moon, comes before Leo, the house of the Sun. Next to these two stand the two houses of Mercury, who is the closest to the Moon—which is the first and lowest planet—while Mercury itself is the second. His houses are Gemini, before Cancer, and Virgo, after Leo. Venus, which is the third planet, is above Mercury and has her houses next to those of Mercury: Taurus before Gemini, and Libra after Virgo. The fourth is the Sun. Mars, which is the fifth, is above Venus and the Sun, and has his houses next to those of Venus: Aries before Taurus, and Scorpio after Libra. Jupiter, which is the sixth, is above Mars and has his houses next to those of Mars: Pisces before Aries, and Sagittarius after Scorpio. Saturn, which is the seventh and highest, is above Jupiter and has his houses next to those of Jupiter: Aquarius before Pisces, and Capricorn after Sagittarius. And they come to be one after...

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Sophia and Philone concerning the astrological relationships between planets and their zodiac houses. **SO.** What do you mean by contrariety? On the contrary, it is friendship and good harmony, because as you yourself have said, Mars is in love with Venus, and both of them get along very well together. **PH.** It is not a contrariety of their influence, like that of Jupiter to Mercury; however, it exists in their **complexion** <note>In Renaissance thought, "complexion" refers to the balance of the four primary qualities: hot, cold, moist, and dry.</note> just as Saturn is contrary to the **Luminaries** <note>The Sun and the Moon.</note>, even though they are also, as I have told you, contrary in influence. However, Mars and Venus are only contrary in their qualitative complexion, for Mars is hot and ardently dry, and Venus is cold and temperately moist—not like the Moon, whose coldness and moisture are excessive. Thus, Mars and Venus harmonize well like two opposites, from whose mixture a temperate effect proceeds, especially in the acts of nutrition and generation; for one provides the heat, which is the active cause in both, and the other provides the temperate moisture, which is their passive cause. And even if the heat of Mars is excessive in its burning, the temperate coldness of Venus tempers it and makes it proportionate to that operation. In this way, in such contrariety consists the amorous suitability of Mars and Venus, and only for that reason do they have their houses opposite one another in the Zodiac. **SO.** I like this explanation for the opposition of the signs based on the hatred or rather the contrariety of the planets to which those houses belong. Tell me, I pray you, if anything of their love and benevolent friendship also appears in the order and opposition, just as the hatred and contrariety appear? **PH.** Yes, it does appear, especially in the Luminaries. You will see that because Jupiter is the **greater fortune** <note>original: *fortuna maggiore*; in astrology, Jupiter is the "Greater Benefic," bringing luck and growth.</note>, none of its houses look upon the houses of the two Luminaries—the Sun and the Moon—with a hostile **aspect** <note>An "aspect" is the angular relationship between planets; some angles are considered harmonious, others stressful.</note>. In contrast, because Saturn is the **greater misfortune** <note>original: *infortunio maggiore*; Saturn is the "Greater Malefic," associated with restriction and decay.</note>, none of its houses look upon those of the Luminaries with a benevolent aspect; rather, they are in opposition, which is totally hostile. But the first house of Jupiter, namely Sagittarius, looks with a **trine aspect** <note>An aspect of 120 degrees, considered the most harmonious and "perfect" relationship between signs.</note> of complete love upon Leo, the house of the Sun, the greater luminary. And the second, namely Pisces, looks upon Cancer, the house of the Moon, the lesser luminary, likewise with a trine aspect of perfect love. Furthermore, none of the houses of Mercury have a hostile aspect with the house of the Sun or with that of the Moon, because it is their most intimate familiar; rather, its first house, which is Gemini, looks with a **sextile aspect** <note>An aspect of 60 degrees, considered a "medium" or friendly harmony.</note> of partial love upon Leo, the house of the Sun, and its second, which is Virgo, looks upon Cancer, the house of the Moon, similarly with a friendly sextile aspect. There remain the houses of Venus, the greater fortune <note>While Jupiter is usually the "Greater Fortune," Philone here groups Venus as a major benefic in relation to the Luminaries.</note>, and of Mars, the **lesser misfortune** <note>original: *infortunio minore*; Mars is the "Lesser Malefic."</note>, which planets, just as they are with s...

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Sophia and Philo. form in a single influence, so equally their houses have a moderate friendship with those of the Sun and the Moon. Aries, the first house of Mars, has a **trine** <note>A trine is a harmonious astrological aspect where planets or signs are 120 degrees apart, usually sharing the same elemental "triplicity" (e.g., Fire).</note> aspect with Leo, the house of the Sun, because both planets and both signs are of the same **complexion** <note>In Renaissance science, the "complexion" is the specific balance of the four qualities: hot, cold, moist, and dry.</note>, being hot and dry. It has a **square** aspect <note>A square is a tense astrological aspect where signs are 90 degrees apart, representing "half-enmity" or friction.</note> of half-enmity with Cancer, the house of the Moon, because they are of contrary qualities. Mars and its house Aries, which are hot and dry, are in opposition to the Moon and its house Cancer, which are cold and moist.

However, Scorpio, the second house of Mars, has a trine aspect of perfect love with Cancer, the house of the Moon, because both signs are of the same complexion—cold and moist. But with Leo, the house of the Sun, it has a square aspect because of their contrariety: Leo being hot and dry, and Scorpio being cold and moist.

And in almost this same manner the houses of Venus are divided in relation to those of the **Luminaries** <note>The Sun and the Moon.</note>, the Sun and Moon. Taurus, the first house of Venus, looks upon Cancer, the house of the Moon, with a friendly **sextile** aspect <note>A sextile is a harmonious aspect where signs are 60 degrees apart.</note> because both are cold. It looks upon Leo, the house of the Sun, with a square aspect of "half-enmity" which is contrary to it, being hot and dry. Similarly, Libra, the second house of Venus, looks upon Leo with a friendly sextile aspect because both are hot; and it looks upon Cancer with a square aspect of half-enmity because it is cold. Thus, these two planets, Mars and Venus, are half-aligned with Saturn and half with Jupiter, and their houses are a mixture of friendship with those of the Sun and the Moon.

I could tell you many other proportions, O Sophia, regarding celestial friendships and enmities, but I wish to leave them aside, for our conversation would become too long and <unclear>difficult</unclear>.

**SO.** Regarding this matter, I only want you to tell me further if the planets have any other sort of friendship and hatred besides their houses being contrary or in good aspect.

**PH.** They have them through good aspects, first by the **exaltation** <note>Exaltation is a placement where a planet's influence is particularly strengthened or "honored," distinct from its home house.</note> of the planets. Each one has a sign in which it has the power of exaltation: the Sun in Aries, the Moon in Taurus, Saturn in Libra, Jupiter in Cancer, Mars in Capricorn, Venus in Pisces, and Mercury in Virgo (even though that is one of its houses). They also have **triplicity** <note>Triplicity refers to the grouping of signs by element (Fire, Earth, Air, Water); certain planets are "lords" or rulers of these groups.</note> authority, which only three planets have in each sign: namely the Sun, Jupiter, and Saturn in the three Fire signs—which are masculine signs—namely Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius. [the Moo]na <note>original: "na", the second half of "Luna" (Moon) continued from the previous page.</note> and Mars have authority in the six feminine signs: that is, in the three earthy signs—**Taurus**, **Virgo**, and **Capricorn**—and in the three watery signs—**Cancer**, **Scorpio**, and **Pisces**.
